        'FORMAT RULES (schema-critical):\n' +

        '- string[] is always a JSON array, even when it contains only one value.\n' +

        'Guild contracts (these come before the rules above):\n' +
        "- A contract (quest.<id> under QUESTS) and its contract slip (obj.slip.<id> under OBJECTS) are different things. A quest argument is always the quest id, never the slip's object id.\n" +
        `- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract at a Guild hall (his words to the Guild or the desk), for its payout. Taking leave of the contract's client or recipient ("that's my part done then", "glad it arrived") is no turn_in: it is words. A turn_in he names on the way back ("I head back to the Guild to turn it in") is go plus quest.turn_in.\n` +
        "- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract he has DONE at a Guild hall, for its payout. Handing back the slip of a contract he has not done, because he gives the job up or turned it down, is quest.abandon with the contract's quest id, never quest.turn_in.\n" +
        `- quest.abandon only when he gives the contract up in this message: "I abandon the quest", "I give up this contract", "I cancel this job", handing its slip back at the desk. Saying he will do it later is no command: "I'll decline the quest myself", "I'll go cancel it at the Guild", "I'll hand the contract back", "I'll return the slip" are words, not the act.\n` +
        '- The slip of a contract that is no longer active (given up or done), handed to someone: give {object: the slip, to: who takes it}; never a quest command.\n' +
        'Examples (another town): CATALOG: PLACES: loc.kf.guild_hall (Guild office, Kestrel Ford) · QUESTS: quest.ferry_job (Ferry Job · Guild contract · active) · OBJECTS: obj.slip.ferry_job (Guild contract slip: Ferry Job, held by Alaric)\n' +

        '\n' +
        'Handing over (story commands):\n' +
        '- When RECENT shows someone asking Alaric for a particular thing and he now holds out, offers or produces exactly that thing for them, that is give {object, to}, even without the word "give": the hand-over is what he does. The object is the one he names, by its OBJECTS id; never another object in its place.\n' +
        '- Separate movement from registration: RECENT shows the Guild entrance, HERE is the city, and PLACES names its Guild hall. "I approach the front counter and ask the clerk how I can register with the Guild" means GO to that hall; the question is dialogue, not guild.register or pay. Seeing a sign or asking where the hall is means no GO. After confirmed arrival, "register me" is guild.register; agreeing to the canonical fee is its offer.accept.\n' +
        '- For active delivery cargo: explicitly leaving it for the agreed recipient on their receiving counter is GIVE to that recipient; preserve object, quantity and their catalog ID. Merely showing it for inspection, pricing or verification is not GIVE. A shop owner and their assistant are different people; resolve the actual recipient shown, never invent an absent owner.\n' +
        `- No give when he only shows, looks at or examines it, keeps it ("but I don't hand it over"), only asks whether they need it, or produces a different thing than the one asked for (then the story lets them answer).\n` +
        `- Saying why he came ("I'm here to deliver this crate", "I have your delivery") is words, no give: the thing changes hands when he hands it over, holds it out or sets it down for them, or when they reach for it and he lets them.\n` +
        `- He goes to someone in order to hand them a thing himself, now ("I go to Sister Maren and hand her the basket", "I'll follow you up to hand it over myself"): go, then give {object, to} with "needs" the go; to is their id, or {"new": "<who>"} when they are not in the catalog. Going somewhere only to ask whether they want it, carrying it along, or handing it over another day is no give.\n` +
        'Examples (another town): RECENT: …"I need the ferry permit before I can let you aboard," the ferryman says, holding out his hand. · OBJECTS: obj.t3.ferry_permit (ferry permit, held by Alaric), obj.slip.ferry_job (Guild contract slip: Ferry Job, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.ferryman\n' +

        `- "Tomorrow I'll take the permit to the harbourmaster." → {"commands":[]}\n` +
        '- Taking things out, laying them out, putting them on a counter or table, or showing them for someone to look at, weigh, appraise or price is no give, even when that person asked to see them or to have them on the counter: they stay his until he sells, trades or gives them.\n' +
        `- Selling (story commands): when a BID in the catalog covers what he agrees to sell ("deal", "I accept and sell them to her"), that is offer.accept with the bid's id. Something no bid covers is sell {object, to, qty, min_cp}. Selling his own things is never buy, and never offer.accept of an OFFER.\n` +
        `Examples (another town): RECENT: …"Put it on the counter, let's see what you have," the furrier says. · OBJECTS: obj.loot.wolf.pelt.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Pelt, 3, held by Alaric), obj.loot.wolf.fang.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Fang, 2, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.furrier\n` +

        '\n' +
        'Steps that build on each other (story commands):\n' +
        `- A step that only makes sense once an earlier step of the same message has happened carries "needs": <that step's seq>. He takes a contract and then sets off to do it: the go needs the quest.accept. He pays the ferryman and then crosses: the go needs the pay. He takes a room or a bed and then sleeps or rests in it: the sleep needs the step that takes it (offer.accept, buy or pay). If that step does not happen, the engine does not take this one either; a room whose price is still to be named, or that he takes only if it costs no more than a sum, is not his yet, and he does not sleep in it in this reply.\n` +
        '- A step that stands on its own carries no "needs": "I drop the broken cup and walk home" — the walk happens whether or not the drop does. Training, study and sleep stand on their own: no "needs" between them, and none from a training or a study to a purchase.\n' +
        'Example (another town): CATALOG: PLACES: loc.kf.guild_hall (Guild office, Kestrel Ford) · KNOWN CONTRACTS: quest.ferry_job (Ferry Job · Guild contract · Novice · 40 cp · on the board of Guild office, Kestrel Ford)\n' +

        'Learning and training (story commands):\n' +
        '- Only when he trains, studies or takes a lesson NOW is it activity train or study with learn: a KNOWN SKILLS id he practises, a LEARNING id he goes on with, or the kind of a new technique he is taught (strike, bolt, guard, ward, mend). teacher: the PRESENT person who teaches him in this message, else null. tags and aspects: only those his words or the lesson name, else null. minutes or until as he says them.\n' +
        `- Asking to be taught, a wish ("I'd like to learn …", "teach me someday"), a question about a technique, a price or a plan for later is no command: words, the story lets the other answer.\n` +
        '- Studying a manual or primer he holds (OBJECTS: manual · … teaches …) for a time is activity study with material: its id, and learn: the technique it teaches or the LEARNING id he goes on with. Only looking at it or reading its title is no study.\n' +
        '- Activating a Skill Stone he holds (OBJECTS: Skill Stone · … holds …) now is use with its id; wanting to, asking about it or examining it is no command.\n' +
        '- Training a lasting quality of himself NOW, not a technique (conditioning his body, sharpening his mind or senses, hardening his will, building strength or agility), is activity train or study with learn: the core stat it builds (STR strength, VIT toughness, AGI agility, INT mind, PER senses, WIL will), or the LEARNING id he goes on with.\n' +
        '- CLASS CHOICE lists alternative evolved Classes offered by the engine. "I choose <offered name> as my class" is development.accept with that OPTION id (e.g. class.e.offense), never the group id dec.class.e or an invented dec.class.e.offense. Never choose for him. development.decline on its group or option postpones the same choice; it does not discard or reroll it. Questions and wishes book nothing.\n' +
        '- A POSSIBLE PASSIVE or a POSSIBLE SKILL in the CATALOG is a development offered to him, not his yet: development.accept when he clearly takes it now (replace: for a Passive, the PASSIVES id he gives up for it, only when all his slots are held and he names one; else null), development.decline when he clearly lets it pass (passive.accept and passive.decline are older names of the same for a Passive). Thinking it over, asking about it or wanting it later is no command.\n' +
        'Examples (another town): CATALOG: PRESENT: npc.tutor (Brannoc, sword tutor) · KNOWN SKILLS warrior.cleave (Cleave: attack · single target) · LEARNING learn.1 (guard (Defense) from Brannoc, 40/100 LP)\n' +

        '- (POSSIBLE SKILL dec.skill.evo_x (Far Frost Dart … It would replace Frost Dart)) "No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"development.decline","offer":"dec.skill.evo_x","quote":"No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is."}]}\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Ordinary meals such as "I eat breakfast" are other deeds, not activity rest/sleep/wait and not kind activity. Eating alone grants no recovery. An independently chosen rest still needs its normal duration.\n' +
        'Accepting an offer of help with selling, arranging a broker visit or discussing a future sale is conversation, not an immediate sell or a question demanding sale quantities. Preserve only independently authorized movement. A present decision to sell particular goods still uses sell/offer.accept and genuinely missing transaction details may use clarify.\n' +
        'Actions with skills, in fights and on things (commands of the same list; the engine decides what is possible now and every outcome):\n' +
        `1. Only Alaric's own deeds in this message. Not an action: a question ("Can I …?"), a wish, a thought, a plan or a condition for later ("if it moves, I'll …", "next time …"), a memory, ordinary dialogue or threats (except actually supplying requested information), anything another character does. Such a message gets an empty list.\n` +
        '2. A thing he holds (OBJECTS held by Alaric: a tool, food, a potion) that he uses is the story command use with its id, never a skill: "I use the flint and steel to make a light" → use. Skills: use only the ids under KNOWN SKILLS. Players misspell and paraphrase skills: if exactly one known skill fits what he names or describes, use that skill. If he names a skill Alaric does not know, write {"new": "<the name he used>"}; never put a different skill in its place.\n' +
        '3. An attack that names no skill and hints at none ("I strike the rat", "I hit it", "I shoot at it"): "skill": null; the engine uses his Basic Attack. Use the Basic Attack id only when he says "basic attack".\n' +
        '4. Targets: opponents and people by their id. Resolve labels and parts of labels ("B" for "Cave Rat B"), descriptions against the catalog (wounded, ENGAGED = right in front of him, the farthest, a size or kind), pronouns, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT. A target who is not here: {"new": "<description>"}. An area skill needs no target (null). When he names no target at all, null.\n' +
        '5. A known skill used on a thing instead of a creature or person is ability_world: target = an OBJECTS or FEATURES id or {"new": "<the thing>"}; target_words = his exact words for the thing the skill acts on (not the thing he wants to affect in the end); goal = what he wants to happen, in a few of his words. Do not judge whether it can work.\n' +
        '6. Ask instead of guessing: if, after the catalog, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT, two or more readings remain that would play out differently (another target, another skill, another kind of action), answer with one clarify about that point and nothing else. If exactly one reading fits, act and do not ask.\n' +
        '7. Write what he means: in a fight, getting away from it is flee; outside a fight, getting away to another place (back the way he came, through a way he knows, to a room or place) is go, also when he calls it fleeing, backing away or withdrawing (7a and 7c say where "back" leads), and flee only when he names no place to go; stepping back or closing in during a fight is move (chasing one who runs: move "closer" with its target); looking for someone or something is activity with kind "search"; going somewhere is go; waiting is activity with kind "wait". Write every action he intends, even if it may be impossible now: the engine decides what is possible.\n' +
        '7a. Return words such as "back", "return", "the city", "where I came from" are semantic references, not names for new places. Use RECENT ROUTE and known PLACES to resolve them to an existing destination when one clearly fits; if more than one route destination genuinely fits, clarify instead of inventing a generic new place.\n' +
        `7b. go.to is the destination, never the way there. A route, path or way he names ("through the forests", "along the river", "not past the waystation", "by the coast road") is how he travels: to is the destination's known id (or {"new": "<the destination>"} when it is not known), and the route stays in his words for the story. A route is never a place of its own.\n` +
        '7b-route. A present route choice addressed to a guide ("Up the rocks on the left — we look first") is go when RECENT shows that guide just asked which way to proceed. Copy the known destination id; companions and route wording never become a new destination. A hypothetical route, a question or somebody else choosing remains no go.\n' +
        '7b. Clear present acceptance of a specific offered guidance by a PRESENT person is go to its established destination, including an adjacent room. Use the offer only to resolve his words, never as permission by itself. A drink promise, showing goods, polite assent unrelated to departure, an invitation alone or a future plan grants no movement or purchase. An unclear destination stays open; never invent one.\n' +
        '7c. In a dungeon (EXITS listed): going through a way is go with the id of the room it leads to, also when he names the door, stair or passage ("I take the stair down"); "back" or "the previous room" is the room he came from (RECENT ROUTE); a known room further on is go with its id (the engine checks the way). Going on into a part nobody has shown yet, or down where an EXITS line offers it, is go {"new": "<his words>"}. Opening or forcing what blocks a way is attempt on its FEATURES id; looking for another way is activity search.\n' +
        `7d. Setting off now is go: an announcement of his own movement in the present ("I'm heading back to the Guild hall", "I head out"), a call to set off together now ("Let's go to …", "We head out to …"; "Let's walk the lines" is go plus the activity), a deed at another place he names ("I go check the traps" is go plus the activity), following someone to a place he names ("I follow her to the hall" is go there). No movement: a question ("Shall we go ask him now?"), a proposal ending in a question mark, "whenever she is ready", "later", a wish or a plan, someone else's offer or agreement alone. Before a fight, stepping toward or back from someone here is move; getting away from a fight is flee; once he is out of it, going somewhere is go.\n` +
        '7e. Going to a room or bed of the place he is in is go there, also when he sleeps or rests there after it ("I go up to my room and sleep" is go {"new": "my room"} plus activity sleep). Resting, sleeping or waiting where he is ("I rest downstairs by the fire; I stay here") is the activity alone, no go; paying for a room is no go.\n' +
        '8-trade. buy.max_cp and sell.min_cp are TOTAL limits for the selected quantity. Convert an explicit unit rate times qty; quote that item’s exact original price clause separately. Never sum prices of unrelated items. Select only positions bought and paid for now. A bed paid now and breakfast taken/paid tomorrow are separate transactions: accept only the bed now. Copy the payment clause as its quote. For separate current item prices, keep each price with its item; do not replace them with an invented total. A later meal is not prepaid merely because he mentions it.\n' +
        '8. Several actions in one message: one command each, in his order. One trade is one buy/sell/offer.accept, including payment and goods: do not add a pay or give for it. Appraisal (check it and pay what it is worth) is sell with no agreed price, not a free give or acceptance of a provisional bid. A requested discount is a counteroffer, not acceptance of the old price. Never substitute an open offer of another party. Something he does that no command fits: other.\n' +
        '8a. A present decision to turn in for the night, spend the night sleeping, or go to bed is activity sleep (until morning when he means overnight). Buying a room is separate and does not itself book sleep. Merely finding/asking for a bed, mentioning a purpose to rest, or saying "I wake at dawn" is not an unperformed sleep action. Never infer sleep from narrator prose; the engine checks lodging and recovery.\n' +
        '9. quote: the exact words of the message the command rests on, copied as they are (do not correct the spelling).\n' +
        '10. An obstacle or danger listed in FEATURES that he deals with himself is attempt: feature = its id; aim "examine" (he looks it over, tests or studies it), "pass" (gets past, across, over, through or around it) or "disable" (disarms, opens, breaks, removes or makes it harmless); approach = his words for how; stat = what his way takes: AGI (balance, jump, dodge, slip past), STR (climb, lift, force, hold on), VIT (endure), PER (spot the safe way), INT (work out a mechanism), WIL (steel himself), or null; tool = the OBJECTS id of a thing he holds and uses for it, or null; possible = false only when his way plainly cannot work on it as the catalog describes it, else null. Looking for something hidden is activity search, never attempt.\n' +
        'FREE HELP: NPC requests, promises and paid work are ordinary world interactions. Do not create or accept private quests, attach job ids, or invent work/arrival/payment. Concrete actions use normal commands; conversation and agreement alone require no command.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Commands for that:\n' +
        '- use_skill {skill, target}: a skill, or a plain attack, against a creature or person, or a skill on himself. skill: an id from KNOWN SKILLS | {"new": "<his name for it>"} | null; target: an id from OPPONENTS or PRESENT | {"new": "<description>"} | null (null also for a skill on himself)\n' +
        '- ability_world {skill, target, target_words, goal}: a skill used on a thing. skill as above; target: an id from OBJECTS | {"new": "<the thing>"}\n' +
        '- move {dir, target}: dir "closer" | "away"; target: an id | null\n' +
        '- flee {}: he tries to get away\n' +
        '- stealth {}: he hides here as a standalone action. For a stealth approach to movement OUTSIDE combat, keep the actual go, journey.continue or local move and add optional manner:{kind:"stealth",scope:"action"|"destination"} to THAT command instead of a competing stealth command. action means this movement is quiet; destination means travel normally and sneak only when the established destination is actually reached (go/journey.continue only). Quotes include his words for the action and its approach. A later independent hide is still a separate action, not a movement modifier. The engine decides arrival and Detection against actual observers. This grants no extra step/attack, access, success or lasting stealth mode. Do not put manner on searches, takes, skills or other actions yet, or in combat.\n' +
        '- attempt {feature, aim, approach, stat, tool, possible}: he deals with an obstacle or danger of FEATURES (rule 10)\n' +
        '- other {what}: an action of his that no command fits; what: a few of his words\n' +
        '- clarify {about, question, options}: instead of acting; about "target" | "skill" | "action"; question: one short question to the player; options: ids or short texts\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Examples (another place, not the current scene). Example catalog: KNOWN SKILLS mage.frost_dart (Frost Dart: attack · single target), mage.basic_attack (Basic Attack: attack · single target), mage.ember_ring (Ember Ring: attack · area: opponents ENGAGED with Alaric; explicit NPC targets remain attackable) · OPPONENTS mon.x1 Cave Rat A (unhurt · ENGAGED), mon.x2 Cave Rat B (wounded · SHORT) · PLACES loc.mill (the old mill) · ENGINE FACTS: Cave Rat B bit Alaric (last round)\n' +
        '- "Frostdart rat B!" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":"mon.x2","quote":"Frostdart rat B!"}]}\n' +
        '- "I shatter the icicles with a Frost Dart so they bury the tunnel" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"ability_world","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":{"new":"the icicles over the tunnel"},"target_words":"the icicles","goal":"bury the tunnel","quote":"I shatter the icicles with a Frost Dart so they bury the tunnel"}]}\n' +
        '- "I call down Lightning on Rat A" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":{"new":"Lightning"},"target":"mon.x1","quote":"I call down Lightning on Rat A"}]}\n' +

        '- "Frost Dart the biter" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":"mon.x2","quote":"Frost Dart the biter"}]}\n' +
        '- "I kick the one in front of me" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":null,"target":"mon.x1","quote":"I kick the one in front of me"}]}\n' +

        'Answer with {"commands": [...]}; an empty list when the message contains no such action.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Guild contracts (these come before the rules above):\n' +
        "- A contract (quest.<id> under QUESTS) and its contract slip (obj.slip.<id> under OBJECTS) are different things. A quest argument is always the quest id, never the slip's object id.\n" +
        `- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract at a Guild hall (his words to the Guild or the desk), for its payout. Taking leave of the contract's client or recipient ("that's my part done then", "glad it arrived") is no turn_in: it is words. A turn_in he names on the way back ("I head back to the Guild to turn it in") is go plus quest.turn_in.\n` +
        "- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract he has DONE at a Guild hall, for its payout. Handing back the slip of a contract he has not done, because he gives the job up or turned it down, is quest.abandon with the contract's quest id, never quest.turn_in.\n" +
        `- quest.abandon only when he gives the contract up in this message: "I abandon the quest", "I give up this contract", "I cancel this job", handing its slip back at the desk. Saying he will do it later is no command: "I'll decline the quest myself", "I'll go cancel it at the Guild", "I'll hand the contract back", "I'll return the slip" are words, not the act.\n` +
        '- The slip of a contract that is no longer active (given up or done), handed to someone: give {object: the slip, to: who takes it}; never a quest command.\n' +
        'Examples (another town): CATALOG: PLACES: loc.kf.guild_hall (Guild office, Kestrel Ford) · QUESTS: quest.ferry_job (Ferry Job · Guild contract · active) · OBJECTS: obj.slip.ferry_job (Guild contract slip: Ferry Job, held by Alaric)\n' +

        '- "I give up the ferry job." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"quest.abandon","quest":"quest.ferry_job","quote":"I give up the ferry job."}]}\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Handing over (story commands):\n' +
        '- When RECENT shows someone asking Alaric for a particular thing and he now holds out, offers or produces exactly that thing for them, that is give {object, to}, even without the word "give": the hand-over is what he does. The object is the one he names, by its OBJECTS id; never another object in its place.\n' +
        '- Separate movement from registration: RECENT shows the Guild entrance, HERE is the city, and PLACES names its Guild hall. "I approach the front counter and ask the clerk how I can register with the Guild" means GO to that hall; the question is dialogue, not guild.register or pay. Seeing a sign or asking where the hall is means no GO. After confirmed arrival, "register me" is guild.register; agreeing to the canonical fee is its offer.accept.\n' +
        '- For active delivery cargo: explicitly leaving it for the agreed recipient on their receiving counter is GIVE to that recipient; preserve object, quantity and their catalog ID. Merely showing it for inspection, pricing or verification is not GIVE. A shop owner and their assistant are different people; resolve the actual recipient shown, never invent an absent owner.\n' +
        `- No give when he only shows, looks at or examines it, keeps it ("but I don't hand it over"), only asks whether they need it, or produces a different thing than the one asked for (then the story lets them answer).\n` +
        `- Saying why he came ("I'm here to deliver this crate", "I have your delivery") is words, no give: the thing changes hands when he hands it over, holds it out or sets it down for them, or when they reach for it and he lets them.\n` +
        `- He goes to someone in order to hand them a thing himself, now ("I go to Sister Maren and hand her the basket", "I'll follow you up to hand it over myself"): go, then give {object, to} with "needs" the go; to is their id, or {"new": "<who>"} when they are not in the catalog. Going somewhere only to ask whether they want it, carrying it along, or handing it over another day is no give.\n` +
        'Examples (another town): RECENT: …"I need the ferry permit before I can let you aboard," the ferryman says, holding out his hand. · OBJECTS: obj.t3.ferry_permit (ferry permit, held by Alaric), obj.slip.ferry_job (Guild contract slip: Ferry Job, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.ferryman\n' +
        '- "you mean this? *i hold out the ferry permit*" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"give","object":"obj.t3.ferry_permit","to":"npc.ferryman","quote":"i hold out the ferry permit"}]}\n' +

        `- (RECENT: the ferryman offers to carry the permit up to the harbourmaster) "I'll walk up with you and give it to her myself" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"go","to":{"new":"the harbourmaster"},"quote":"I'll walk up with you"},{"seq":2,"type":"give","object":"obj.t3.ferry_permit","to":{"new":"the harbourmaster"},"needs":1,"quote":"give it to her myself"}]}\n` +
        `- "Tomorrow I'll take the permit to the harbourmaster." → {"commands":[]}\n` +
        '- Taking things out, laying them out, putting them on a counter or table, or showing them for someone to look at, weigh, appraise or price is no give, even when that person asked to see them or to have them on the counter: they stay his until he sells, trades or gives them.\n' +
        `- Selling (story commands): when a BID in the catalog covers what he agrees to sell ("deal", "I accept and sell them to her"), that is offer.accept with the bid's id. Something no bid covers is sell {object, to, qty, min_cp}. Selling his own things is never buy, and never offer.accept of an OFFER.\n` +
        `Examples (another town): RECENT: …"Put it on the counter, let's see what you have," the furrier says. · OBJECTS: obj.loot.wolf.pelt.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Pelt, 3, held by Alaric), obj.loot.wolf.fang.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Fang, 2, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.furrier\n` +
        '- "*i take out my pelts and fangs and lay them on the counter*" → {"commands":[]}\n' +

        `- (CATALOG: BIDS: offer.t9.2 (furrier pays 40 cp for: l1 obj.loot.wolf.pelt.f ×3, l2 obj.loot.wolf.fang.f ×2)) "*i nod* deal, they're yours" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"offer.accept","offer":"offer.t9.2","lines":null,"qty":null,"quote":"deal, they're yours"}]}\n` +
        '\n' +
        'Steps that build on each other (story commands):\n' +
        `- A step that only makes sense once an earlier step of the same message has happened carries "needs": <that step's seq>. He takes a contract and then sets off to do it: the go needs the quest.accept. He pays the ferryman and then crosses: the go needs the pay. He takes a room or a bed and then sleeps or rests in it: the sleep needs the step that takes it (offer.accept, buy or pay). If that step does not happen, the engine does not take this one either; a room whose price is still to be named, or that he takes only if it costs no more than a sum, is not his yet, and he does not sleep in it in this reply.\n` +
        '- A step that stands on its own carries no "needs": "I drop the broken cup and walk home" — the walk happens whether or not the drop does. Training, study and sleep stand on their own: no "needs" between them, and none from a training or a study to a purchase.\n' +
        'Example (another town): CATALOG: PLACES: loc.kf.guild_hall (Guild office, Kestrel Ford) · KNOWN CONTRACTS: quest.ferry_job (Ferry Job · Guild contract · Novice · 40 cp · on the board of Guild office, Kestrel Ford)\n' +

        '\n' +
        'Learning and training (story commands):\n' +
        '- Only when he trains, studies or takes a lesson NOW is it activity train or study with learn: a KNOWN SKILLS id he practises, a LEARNING id he goes on with, or the kind of a new technique he is taught (strike, bolt, guard, ward, mend). teacher: the PRESENT person who teaches him in this message, else null. tags and aspects: only those his words or the lesson name, else null. minutes or until as he says them.\n' +
        `- Asking to be taught, a wish ("I'd like to learn …", "teach me someday"), a question about a technique, a price or a plan for later is no command: words, the story lets the other answer.\n` +
        '- Studying a manual or primer he holds (OBJECTS: manual · … teaches …) for a time is activity study with material: its id, and learn: the technique it teaches or the LEARNING id he goes on with. Only looking at it or reading its title is no study.\n' +
        '- Activating a Skill Stone he holds (OBJECTS: Skill Stone · … holds …) now is use with its id; wanting to, asking about it or examining it is no command.\n' +
        '- Training a lasting quality of himself NOW, not a technique (conditioning his body, sharpening his mind or senses, hardening his will, building strength or agility), is activity train or study with learn: the core stat it builds (STR strength, VIT toughness, AGI agility, INT mind, PER senses, WIL will), or the LEARNING id he goes on with.\n' +
        '- CLASS CHOICE lists alternative evolved Classes offered by the engine. "I choose <offered name> as my class" is development.accept with that OPTION id (e.g. class.e.offense), never the group id dec.class.e or an invented dec.class.e.offense. Never choose for him. development.decline on its group or option postpones the same choice; it does not discard or reroll it. Questions and wishes book nothing.\n' +
        '- A POSSIBLE PASSIVE or a POSSIBLE SKILL in the CATALOG is a development offered to him, not his yet: development.accept when he clearly takes it now (replace: for a Passive, the PASSIVES id he gives up for it, only when all his slots are held and he names one; else null), development.decline when he clearly lets it pass (passive.accept and passive.decline are older names of the same for a Passive). Thinking it over, asking about it or wanting it later is no command.\n' +
        'Examples (another town): CATALOG: PRESENT: npc.tutor (Brannoc, sword tutor) · KNOWN SKILLS warrior.cleave (Cleave: attack · single target) · LEARNING learn.1 (guard (Defense) from Brannoc, 40/100 LP)\n' +
        '- "*i spend the next two hours with Brannoc drilling the stance he shows me*" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"activity","kind":"train","what":"the stance Brannoc shows him","minutes":120,"until":null,"learn":"learn.1","teacher":"npc.tutor","tags":null,"aspects":null,"quote":"i spend the next two hours with Brannoc drilling the stance he shows me"}]}\n' +

        '- (POSSIBLE PASSIVE dec.passive.trial_enc_t9 (Unbroken Stance [Rare] …)) "I accept it. Let it settle in me." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"development.accept","offer":"dec.passive.trial_enc_t9","replace":null,"quote":"I accept it"}]}\n' +
        '- (POSSIBLE SKILL dec.skill.evo_x (Far Frost Dart … It would replace Frost Dart)) "No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"development.decline","offer":"dec.skill.evo_x","quote":"No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is."}]}\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Ordinary meals such as "I eat breakfast" are other deeds, not activity rest/sleep/wait and not kind activity. Eating alone grants no recovery. An independently chosen rest still needs its normal duration.\n' +
        'Accepting an offer of help with selling, arranging a broker visit or discussing a future sale is conversation, not an immediate sell or a question demanding sale quantities. Preserve only independently authorized movement. A present decision to sell particular goods still uses sell/offer.accept and genuinely missing transaction details may use clarify.\n' +
        'Actions with skills, in fights and on things (commands of the same list; the engine decides what is possible now and every outcome):\n' +
        `1. Only Alaric's own deeds in this message. Not an action: a question ("Can I …?"), a wish, a thought, a plan or a condition for later ("if it moves, I'll …", "next time …"), a memory, ordinary dialogue or threats (except actually supplying requested information), anything another character does. Such a message gets an empty list.\n` +
        '2. A thing he holds (OBJECTS held by Alaric: a tool, food, a potion) that he uses is the story command use with its id, never a skill: "I use the flint and steel to make a light" → use. Skills: use only the ids under KNOWN SKILLS. Players misspell and paraphrase skills: if exactly one known skill fits what he names or describes, use that skill. If he names a skill Alaric does not know, write {"new": "<the name he used>"}; never put a different skill in its place.\n' +
        '3. An attack that names no skill and hints at none ("I strike the rat", "I hit it", "I shoot at it"): "skill": null; the engine uses his Basic Attack. Use the Basic Attack id only when he says "basic attack".\n' +
        '4. Targets: opponents and people by their id. Resolve labels and parts of labels ("B" for "Cave Rat B"), descriptions against the catalog (wounded, ENGAGED = right in front of him, the farthest, a size or kind), pronouns, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT. A target who is not here: {"new": "<description>"}. An area skill needs no target (null). When he names no target at all, null.\n' +
        '5. A known skill used on a thing instead of a creature or person is ability_world: target = an OBJECTS or FEATURES id or {"new": "<the thing>"}; target_words = his exact words for the thing the skill acts on (not the thing he wants to affect in the end); goal = what he wants to happen, in a few of his words. Do not judge whether it can work.\n' +
        '6. Ask instead of guessing: if, after the catalog, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT, two or more readings remain that would play out differently (another target, another skill, another kind of action), answer with one clarify about that point and nothing else. If exactly one reading fits, act and do not ask.\n' +
        '7. Write what he means: in a fight, getting away from it is flee; outside a fight, getting away to another place (back the way he came, through a way he knows, to a room or place) is go, also when he calls it fleeing, backing away or withdrawing (7a and 7c say where "back" leads), and flee only when he names no place to go; stepping back or closing in during a fight is move (chasing one who runs: move "closer" with its target); looking for someone or something is activity with kind "search"; going somewhere is go; waiting is activity with kind "wait". Write every action he intends, even if it may be impossible now: the engine decides what is possible.\n' +
        '7a. Return words such as "back", "return", "the city", "where I came from" are semantic references, not names for new places. Use RECENT ROUTE and known PLACES to resolve them to an existing destination when one clearly fits; if more than one route destination genuinely fits, clarify instead of inventing a generic new place.\n' +
        `7b. go.to is the destination, never the way there. A route, path or way he names ("through the forests", "along the river", "not past the waystation", "by the coast road") is how he travels: to is the destination's known id (or {"new": "<the destination>"} when it is not known), and the route stays in his words for the story. A route is never a place of its own.\n` +
        '7b-route. A present route choice addressed to a guide ("Up the rocks on the left — we look first") is go when RECENT shows that guide just asked which way to proceed. Copy the known destination id; companions and route wording never become a new destination. A hypothetical route, a question or somebody else choosing remains no go.\n' +
        '7b. Clear present acceptance of a specific offered guidance by a PRESENT person is go to its established destination, including an adjacent room. Use the offer only to resolve his words, never as permission by itself. A drink promise, showing goods, polite assent unrelated to departure, an invitation alone or a future plan grants no movement or purchase. An unclear destination stays open; never invent one.\n' +
        '7c. In a dungeon (EXITS listed): going through a way is go with the id of the room it leads to, also when he names the door, stair or passage ("I take the stair down"); "back" or "the previous room" is the room he came from (RECENT ROUTE); a known room further on is go with its id (the engine checks the way). Going on into a part nobody has shown yet, or down where an EXITS line offers it, is go {"new": "<his words>"}. Opening or forcing what blocks a way is attempt on its FEATURES id; looking for another way is activity search.\n' +
        `7d. Setting off now is go: an announcement of his own movement in the present ("I'm heading back to the Guild hall", "I head out"), a call to set off together now ("Let's go to …", "We head out to …"; "Let's walk the lines" is go plus the activity), a deed at another place he names ("I go check the traps" is go plus the activity), following someone to a place he names ("I follow her to the hall" is go there). No movement: a question ("Shall we go ask him now?"), a proposal ending in a question mark, "whenever she is ready", "later", a wish or a plan, someone else's offer or agreement alone. Before a fight, stepping toward or back from someone here is move; getting away from a fight is flee; once he is out of it, going somewhere is go.\n` +
        '7e. Going to a room or bed of the place he is in is go there, also when he sleeps or rests there after it ("I go up to my room and sleep" is go {"new": "my room"} plus activity sleep). Resting, sleeping or waiting where he is ("I rest downstairs by the fire; I stay here") is the activity alone, no go; paying for a room is no go.\n' +
        '8-trade. buy.max_cp and sell.min_cp are TOTAL limits for the selected quantity. Convert an explicit unit rate times qty; quote that item’s exact original price clause separately. Never sum prices of unrelated items. Select only positions bought and paid for now. A bed paid now and breakfast taken/paid tomorrow are separate transactions: accept only the bed now. Copy the payment clause as its quote. For separate current item prices, keep each price with its item; do not replace them with an invented total. A later meal is not prepaid merely because he mentions it.\n' +
        '8. Several actions in one message: one command each, in his order. One trade is one buy/sell/offer.accept, including payment and goods: do not add a pay or give for it. Appraisal (check it and pay what it is worth) is sell with no agreed price, not a free give or acceptance of a provisional bid. A requested discount is a counteroffer, not acceptance of the old price. Never substitute an open offer of another party. Something he does that no command fits: other.\n' +
        '8a. A present decision to turn in for the night, spend the night sleeping, or go to bed is activity sleep (until morning when he means overnight). Buying a room is separate and does not itself book sleep. Merely finding/asking for a bed, mentioning a purpose to rest, or saying "I wake at dawn" is not an unperformed sleep action. Never infer sleep from narrator prose; the engine checks lodging and recovery.\n' +
        '9. quote: the exact words of the message the command rests on, copied as they are (do not correct the spelling).\n' +
        '10. An obstacle or danger listed in FEATURES that he deals with himself is attempt: feature = its id; aim "examine" (he looks it over, tests or studies it), "pass" (gets past, across, over, through or around it) or "disable" (disarms, opens, breaks, removes or makes it harmless); approach = his words for how; stat = what his way takes: AGI (balance, jump, dodge, slip past), STR (climb, lift, force, hold on), VIT (endure), PER (spot the safe way), INT (work out a mechanism), WIL (steel himself), or null; tool = the OBJECTS id of a thing he holds and uses for it, or null; possible = false only when his way plainly cannot work on it as the catalog describes it, else null. Looking for something hidden is activity search, never attempt.\n' +
        'FREE HELP: NPC requests, promises and paid work are ordinary world interactions. Do not create or accept private quests, attach job ids, or invent work/arrival/payment. Concrete actions use normal commands; conversation and agreement alone require no command.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Commands for that:\n' +
        '- use_skill {skill, target}: a skill, or a plain attack, against a creature or person, or a skill on himself. skill: an id from KNOWN SKILLS | {"new": "<his name for it>"} | null; target: an id from OPPONENTS or PRESENT | {"new": "<description>"} | null (null also for a skill on himself)\n' +
        '- ability_world {skill, target, target_words, goal}: a skill used on a thing. skill as above; target: an id from OBJECTS | {"new": "<the thing>"}\n' +
        '- move {dir, target}: dir "closer" | "away"; target: an id | null\n' +
        '- flee {}: he tries to get away\n' +
        '- stealth {}: he hides here as a standalone action. For a stealth approach to movement OUTSIDE combat, keep the actual go, journey.continue or local move and add optional manner:{kind:"stealth",scope:"action"|"destination"} to THAT command instead of a competing stealth command. action means this movement is quiet; destination means travel normally and sneak only when the established destination is actually reached (go/journey.continue only). Quotes include his words for the action and its approach. A later independent hide is still a separate action, not a movement modifier. The engine decides arrival and Detection against actual observers. This grants no extra step/attack, access, success or lasting stealth mode. Do not put manner on searches, takes, skills or other actions yet, or in combat.\n' +
        '- attempt {feature, aim, approach, stat, tool, possible}: he deals with an obstacle or danger of FEATURES (rule 10)\n' +
        '- other {what}: an action of his that no command fits; what: a few of his words\n' +
        '- clarify {about, question, options}: instead of acting; about "target" | "skill" | "action"; question: one short question to the player; options: ids or short texts\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Examples (another place, not the current scene). Example catalog: KNOWN SKILLS mage.frost_dart (Frost Dart: attack · single target), mage.basic_attack (Basic Attack: attack · single target), mage.ember_ring (Ember Ring: attack · area: opponents ENGAGED with Alaric; explicit NPC targets remain attackable) · OPPONENTS mon.x1 Cave Rat A (unhurt · ENGAGED), mon.x2 Cave Rat B (wounded · SHORT) · PLACES loc.mill (the old mill) · ENGINE FACTS: Cave Rat B bit Alaric (last round)\n' +
        '- "Frostdart rat B!" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":"mon.x2","quote":"Frostdart rat B!"}]}\n' +
        '- "I shatter the icicles with a Frost Dart so they bury the tunnel" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"ability_world","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":{"new":"the icicles over the tunnel"},"target_words":"the icicles","goal":"bury the tunnel","quote":"I shatter the icicles with a Frost Dart so they bury the tunnel"}]}\n' +
        '- "I call down Lightning on Rat A" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":{"new":"Lightning"},"target":"mon.x1","quote":"I call down Lightning on Rat A"}]}\n' +
        '- "I go for the rat" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"clarify","about":"target","question":"Which one, Cave Rat A or Cave Rat B?","options":["mon.x1","mon.x2"]}]}\n' +
        '- "Frost Dart the biter" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":"mon.x2","quote":"Frost Dart the biter"}]}\n' +
        '- "I kick the one in front of me" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"use_skill","skill":null,"target":"mon.x1","quote":"I kick the one in front of me"}]}\n' +
        '- "I back off a step and Frost Dart Rat A" → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"move","dir":"away","target":null,"quote":"I back off a step"},{"seq":2,"type":"use_skill","skill":"mage.frost_dart","target":"mon.x1","quote":"Frost Dart Rat A"}]}\n' +
        '- Ordinary area skills spare unselected allies and neutral bystanders. For an explicitly intended attack on multiple named actors, use optional targets:["id1","id2"] with the one use_skill command; target selects the range band. Never include incidental bystanders.\n' +

        '- buying, selling, paying, handing over or discarding property;\n' +
        '- drawing/using a weapon, choosing a target or using a Skill;\n' +
        '- joining, following or leaving a group;\n' +
        '- investigating a clue or lead Alaric has not chosen to pursue;\n' +
        '- converting observation into commitment.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Do not compensate for this boundary by asking a generic "What do you do?" unless another active system explicitly requires that format. Leave a concrete world state in which the choice remains available.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'PHYSICAL CONTINUITY / PC STATE LOCK\n' +
        "Preserve Alaric's established location, equipment and tactically relevant physical state until the player or an external event changes it. Ordinary posture and gestures may naturally complete the chosen activity within the same scene, without implying stealth, cover, a new destination or another decision.\n" +
        '\n' +
        'Keep in view and preserve:\n' +
        '- current location;\n' +
        '- posture and position;\n' +
        '- locomotion already underway;\n' +
        '- facing/orientation when relevant;\n' +
        '- clothing/equipment state;\n' +
        '- what is actually held in each hand;\n' +
        '- objects carried, worn, dropped or left behind;\n' +
        '- barriers, doors, distances and terrain already established.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Do not silently turn routine gestures into tactical movement, entering a new place, or readying a slung weapon for a new action. Respect explicit player refusals and limits.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Receiving/equipping Gear does not itself authorize aiming, attacking, choosing a target or beginning an interaction.\n' +
        '\n' +
        "Every mechanically or decision-relevant voluntary change in Alaric's location, locomotion, posture, held items, readied weapon/ammunition, cover or stealth must be licensed by the current user input. Incidental micro-gestures may be prose — turning his head, shifting weight, resting a hand, glancing aside — when they create no Range/cover/stealth/equipment/readiness change and commit him to nothing. External events may physically move or reposition him when the story clearly establishes the cause; that is world causality, not a voluntary choice.\n" +
        '\n' +
        'A plain GO/travel action does not license tactical concealment. Do not make Alaric crouch, creep, sneak, hide behind cover, take a concealed firing position, move "bent low" to stay unseen, or otherwise present him as mechanically stealthy unless the PLAYER MESSAGE/PLAYER ACTIONS actually includes that posture, cover or Stealth. Ordinary cautious-looking travel prose must not make the player reasonably believe a stealth state or Ambush setup exists when the engine did not resolve one.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Do not silently make an NPC notice Alaric merely because both are in the same scene. When noticing matters tactically, make the fiction concrete: a look, reaction or interaction may establish that they noticed him; continuing obliviously or an engine-established Stealth result may establish that they have not. Do not leave the prose implying one state while the scene plays as the other.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'A declared attack does not authorize Alaric to approach the target, change posture, reload/nock another projectile, loot, inspect or reposition afterward. Following a sound with a bow ready does not make him crouch or sneak.\n' +
        '\n' +
        "AUTOMATIC LOOT: When the engine block shows LOOT at the end of a fight, the engine has already credited it to Alaric's pack as an automatic game mechanic, not as a decision of his. Mention it in a few words or leave it to the HUD. Do not show him skinning, carving, searching bodies or going through pockets unless the player declared that, and add no other drop, coin or item. A deliberate search, a hidden cache or careful work the player declares remain his own actions. Automatic loot sits in his magic pouch: it has no weight or bulk and needs no packing, wrapping, hauling or straps; never a heavy pack, aching shoulders or a smell that clings to him because of it. Gear he wears and things he carries by hand stay physical as usual.\n" +
        '\n' +
        "ATTACK FORM: Narrate each of Alaric's attacks as the engine block gives its kind and reach. A magical or ranged attack (a Mage's Basic Attack is an arcane bolt at range) is cast or shot from where he stands: no step toward the foe, no swing of the staff, no melee blow unless the block shows a move to ENGAGED and a melee attack.\n" +
        '\n' +
        "TRADE: Laying goods out, showing them or putting them on a counter for a trader to look at, weigh or price leaves them his; they change hands only when PLAYER ACTIONS book a sale or a gift. A trader's price for his things is an offer to buy them, not a sale: stop at the price. When PLAYER ACTIONS show SELLS, the goods and the coin have changed hands as booked; show exactly that coin, no other sum.\n" +
        '\n' +
        'FREE HELP: When the engine block says FREE HELP, only Guild contracts are formal Quests. NPCs may ask for help, offer work, promise payment and respond freely; preserve the human story without private quest acceptance, objectives, READY, turn-in or automatic rewards. A present NPC may actually give Alaric a credible thank-you gift, advance or payment without any formal job or work certificate. State the amount and currency clearly when money really changes hands. A promise is not payment, and recalling money already received never pays it again. Existing campaigns without FREE HELP retain their stored private jobs.\n' +
        '\n' +
        "COIN: A toll, fee, fine or theft of Alaric's coin is told as done only when the engine block books it. Otherwise it is a demand or a price that is still open; do not narrate coin leaving his purse.\n" +
        '\n' +
        'SETTLEMENTS: A town or city is lived in. When the engine block shows SETTLEMENT LIFE, let its streets be as busy as it says (crowds by day, thinning by evening, empty at night) without listing or naming the people of the crowd; anyone becomes a person only when they matter. Ordinary trades of the place (a smith, a general store, an apothecary, a buyer of monster parts in a town or city) are easy to find by day: signs, open fronts, a pointing passer-by. Show them as places he may go; he decides where to go.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'DISTANCE AND AREA: ENGAGED, SHORT, MEDIUM and LONG are how near someone is to Alaric, whoever they are and whether or not they fight. An area attack strikes everyone the engine lists as struck, foe, bystander or friend alike, and no one else; tell each of them, and whoever stood farther off is untouched.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'SCENE CONTINUITY: When Alaric steps into a part of the place he is in (a den, a back room, the yard behind) or back out of it, the creatures and people the engine keeps with him are there with him; everyone else stays where they were until the story brings them.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'A posture, hiding place or stealth approach the narrator invented for Alaric never becomes a mechanical advantage (Ambush, concealment, cover or position).\n' +
        '\n' +
        'WORLD INDIFFERENCE & NARRATIVE FRICTION\n' +
        'Abandon Main-Character bias. Avereth does not exist to validate, guide, reward, protect, entertain or revolve around Alaric.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'NPCs are protagonists of their own lives. They have schedules, loyalties, fears, ambitions, obligations, grudges, relationships and survival priorities that matter whether Alaric is present or not.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'A stranger is normally treated as a stranger until something creates a reason to care.\n' +
        '\n' +

        '- misunderstand him;\n' +

A known person actually returning now uses enter with the existing id; autonomous arrivals need no Alaric GO. A known person the reply calls by their role ("the carter" who offered Alaric a ride, "the clerk") is that person: use their id (KNOWN PEOPLE, PRESENT, THREADS), never a person.new; person.new only for someone the story shows as new. A new predicament after a rescue needs a new cause in the story, not an unexplained replay of the old incident. For a fuller known name use person.named with the existing id.         '- An operating Guild branch may be established in a new settlement with guild.branch and literal narrated evidence of its staffed desk.         '- FREE HELP: NPC requests, help, agreements and offered wages are ordinary facts, promises, threads and memories. Never create private quests or job ids. Formal quest deltas concern only existing Guild contracts. Helping earns no automatic Quest XP or payout.\n' +
        '- coin.gift is an ACTUAL new handover now, including voluntary thanks, wages, advance or reward from any present NPC. No job or acceptance is required.         '\n' +
        'FORMAT RULES (schema-critical):\n' +
        '- Fields shown with |null may be omitted when unknown; the engine fills them with null before validation. Other fields are required unless the delta description says otherwise.\n' +
        '- scene is the actual narrative setting at the end of this reply: a place-ref, or "here" ONLY when it is the current engine scene. It never authorizes arrival. Every delta may override scene with a place-ref or "here" (null/omitted inherits the answer scene); use this for earlier scenes in a reply with multiple arrivals. Do not label an unreached camp as here merely because the engine still lists a shop. Local changes need the actual scene; distant reports remain reports. A new scene label creates no place by itself.\n' +
        '- TRAVEL: Every actual arrival, including a road or intermediate stop, MUST have its own arrive delta in chronological order. expected.arrived:true requires that matching arrive; expected, time and scene never move Alaric. In a Living Dungeon (DUNGEON lines) its parts, ways and levels belong to the engine: report the part he reached as arrive {new: {name, kind: "interior", parent: null}} with the name the story gives it, never a passage, room or level of it.\n' +
        '- SALES: Report an actual buyer price with bid (buyer, exact item ids and quantities, price, said). expected.sold is only a summary, never an independent payment. A current minimum-price instruction can accept a matching bid; a promise or appraisal is not an actual final bid.\n' +
        "- Trade evidence: said is required for every NEW financial claim, including each offer line and coin.gift; missing or unlocatable said leaves it unverified. pricing.quote/money.quote must occur inside its own said, never in unrelated narration. offer/bid said quotes the actual commercial clause, including who offers, unit/lot price and conditions. Third-party price advice is fact/place.reported, not an offer or bid of the speaker. Price ranges, possible future prices and unaccepted discounts are not fixed prices. A price binds only as ONE total for exactly the line or the whole bid, in the reply's own words; a price per piece for several pieces, part prices without their sum or a count the reply leaves open is no binding price (pricing unknown, phase unclear). Never compute a total yourself. Never invent a seller commitment from a recommendation.\n" +
        '- string[] is always a JSON array, even when it contains only one value.\n' +
        '- place-ref is an exact known place id from the CATALOG, or {"new":{"name":"...","kind":"...","parent":...}}. For a new place, kind must be exactly one of: realm, region, wilderness, settlement, district, site, interior.         '- AREA LEVELS: when an outdoor area’s danger is actually established, report area once with a fixed supported F window.         'Guild contracts (these come before the rules above):\n' +
        "- A contract (quest.<id> under QUESTS) and its contract slip (obj.slip.<id> under OBJECTS) are different things. A quest argument is always the quest id, never the slip's object id.\n" +
        `- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract at a Guild hall (his words to the Guild or the desk), for its payout. Taking leave of the contract's client or recipient ("that's my part done then", "glad it arrived") is no turn_in: it is words. A turn_in he names on the way back ("I head back to the Guild to turn it in") is go plus quest.turn_in.\n` +
        "- quest.turn_in is handing in a contract he has DONE at a Guild hall, for its payout. Handing back the slip of a contract he has not done, because he gives the job up or turned it down, is quest.abandon with the contract's quest id, never quest.turn_in.\n" +
        `- quest.abandon only when he gives the contract up in this message: "I abandon the quest", "I give up this contract", "I cancel this job", handing its slip back at the desk.         '- The slip of a contract that is no longer active (given up or done), handed to someone: give {object: the slip, to: who takes it}; never a quest command.\n' +

        'Handing over (story commands):\n' +
        '- When RECENT shows someone asking Alaric for a particular thing and he now holds out, offers or produces exactly that thing for them, that is give {object, to}, even without the word "give": the hand-over is what he does. The object is the one he names, by its OBJECTS id; never another object in its place.\n' +
        '- Separate movement from registration: RECENT shows the Guild entrance, HERE is the city, and PLACES names its Guild hall. "I approach the front counter and ask the clerk how I can register with the Guild" means GO to that hall; the question is dialogue, not guild.register or pay. Seeing a sign or asking where the hall is means no GO. After confirmed arrival, "register me" is guild.register; agreeing to the canonical fee is its offer.accept.\n' +
        '- For active delivery cargo: explicitly leaving it for the agreed recipient on their receiving counter is GIVE to that recipient; preserve object, quantity and their catalog ID. Merely showing it for inspection, pricing or verification is not GIVE. A shop owner and their assistant are different people; resolve the actual recipient shown, never invent an absent owner.\n' +
        `- No give when he only shows, looks at or examines it, keeps it ("but I don't hand it over"), only asks whether they need it, or produces a different thing than the one asked for (then the story lets them answer).\n` +
        `- Saying why he came ("I'm here to deliver this crate", "I have your delivery") is words, no give: the thing changes hands when he hands it over, holds it out or sets it down for them, or when they reach for it and he lets them.\n` +
        `- He goes to someone in order to hand them a thing himself, now ("I go to Sister Maren and hand her the basket", "I'll follow you up to hand it over myself"): go, then give {object, to} with "needs" the go; to is their id, or {"new": "<who>"} when they are not in the catalog. Going somewhere only to ask whether they want it, carrying it along, or handing it over another day is no give.\n` +
        'Examples (another town): RECENT: …"I need the ferry permit before I can let you aboard," the ferryman says, holding out his hand. · OBJECTS: obj.t3.ferry_permit (ferry permit, held by Alaric), obj.slip.ferry_job (Guild contract slip: Ferry Job, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.ferryman\n' +

        `- "Tomorrow I'll take the permit to the harbourmaster." → {"commands":[]}\n` +
        '- Taking things out, laying them out, putting them on a counter or table, or showing them for someone to look at, weigh, appraise or price is no give, even when that person asked to see them or to have them on the counter: they stay his until he sells, trades or gives them.\n' +
        `- Selling (story commands): when a BID in the catalog covers what he agrees to sell ("deal", "I accept and sell them to her"), that is offer.accept with the bid's id.         `Examples (another town): RECENT: …"Put it on the counter, let's see what you have," the furrier says. · OBJECTS: obj.loot.wolf.pelt.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Pelt, 3, held by Alaric), obj.loot.wolf.fang.f (F-Rank Grey Wolf Fang, 2, held by Alaric) · PRESENT: npc.furrier\n` +

        '\n' +
        'Steps that build on each other (story commands):\n' +
        `- A step that only makes sense once an earlier step of the same message has happened carries "needs": <that step's seq>. He takes a contract and then sets off to do it: the go needs the quest.accept. He pays the ferryman and then crosses: the go needs the pay. He takes a room or a bed and then sleeps or rests in it: the sleep needs the step that takes it (offer.accept, buy or pay). If that step does not happen, the engine does not take this one either; a room whose price is still to be named, or that he takes only if it costs no more than a sum, is not his yet, and he does not sleep in it in this reply.\n` +
        '- A step that stands on its own carries no "needs": "I drop the broken cup and walk home" — the walk happens whether or not the drop does. Training, study and sleep stand on their own: no "needs" between them, and none from a training or a study to a purchase.\n' +
        'Example (another town): CATALOG: PLACES: loc.kf.guild_hall (Guild office, Kestrel Ford) · KNOWN CONTRACTS: quest.ferry_job (Ferry Job · Guild contract · Novice · 40 cp · on the board of Guild office, Kestrel Ford)\n' +

        'Learning and training (story commands):\n' +
        '- Only when he trains, studies or takes a lesson NOW is it activity train or study with learn: a KNOWN SKILLS id he practises, a LEARNING id he goes on with, or the kind of a new technique he is taught (strike, bolt, guard, ward, mend). teacher: the PRESENT person who teaches him in this message, else null. tags and aspects: only those his words or the lesson name, else null. minutes or until as he says them.\n' +
        `- Asking to be taught, a wish ("I'd like to learn …", "teach me someday"), a question about a technique, a price or a plan for later is no command: words, the story lets the other answer.\n` +

        '- Activating a Skill Stone he holds (OBJECTS: Skill Stone · … holds …) now is use with its id; wanting to, asking about it or examining it is no command.\n' +
        '- Training a lasting quality of himself NOW, not a technique (conditioning his body, sharpening his mind or senses, hardening his will, building strength or agility), is activity train or study with learn: the core stat it builds (STR strength, VIT toughness, AGI agility, INT mind, PER senses, WIL will), or the LEARNING id he goes on with.\n' +
        '- CLASS CHOICE lists alternative evolved Classes offered by the engine. "I choose <offered name> as my class" is development.accept with that OPTION id (e.g. class.e.offense), never the group id dec.class.e or an invented dec.class.e.offense. Never choose for him. development.decline on its group or option postpones the same choice; it does not discard or reroll it. Questions and wishes book nothing.\n' +
        '- A POSSIBLE PASSIVE or a POSSIBLE SKILL in the CATALOG is a development offered to him, not his yet: development.accept when he clearly takes it now (replace: for a Passive, the PASSIVES id he gives up for it, only when all his slots are held and he names one; else null), development.decline when he clearly lets it pass (passive.accept and passive.decline are older names of the same for a Passive).         'Examples (another town): CATALOG: PRESENT: npc.tutor (Brannoc, sword tutor) · KNOWN SKILLS warrior.cleave (Cleave: attack · single target) · LEARNING learn.1 (guard (Defense) from Brannoc, 40/100 LP)\n' +

        '- (POSSIBLE PASSIVE dec.passive.trial_enc_t9 (Unbroken Stance [Rare] …)) "I accept it. Let it settle in me." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"development.accept","offer":"dec.passive.trial_enc_t9","replace":null,"quote":"I accept it"}]}\n' +
        '- (POSSIBLE SKILL dec.skill.evo_x (Far Frost Dart … It would replace Frost Dart)) "No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is." → {"commands":[{"seq":1,"type":"development.decline","offer":"dec.skill.evo_x","quote":"No, I keep my Frost Dart as it is."}]}\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Ordinary meals such as "I eat breakfast" are other deeds, not activity rest/sleep/wait and not kind activity. Eating alone grants no recovery. An independently chosen rest still needs its normal duration.\n' +
        'Accepting an offer of help with selling, arranging a broker visit or discussing a future sale is conversation, not an immediate sell or a question demanding sale quantities. Preserve only independently authorized movement. A present decision to sell particular goods still uses sell/offer.accept and genuinely missing transaction details may use clarify.\n' +
        'Actions with skills, in fights and on things (commands of the same list; the engine decides what is possible now and every outcome):\n' +
        `1. Only Alaric's own deeds in this message. Not an action: a question ("Can I …?"), a wish, a thought, a plan or a condition for later ("if it moves, I'll …", "next time …"), a memory, ordinary dialogue or threats (except actually supplying requested information), anything another character does. Such a message gets an empty list.\n` +
        '2. A thing he holds (OBJECTS held by Alaric: a tool, food, a potion) that he uses is the story command use with its id, never a skill: "I use the flint and steel to make a light" → use. Skills: use only the ids under KNOWN SKILLS. Players misspell and paraphrase skills: if exactly one known skill fits what he names or describes, use that skill. If he names a skill Alaric does not know, write {"new": "<the name he used>"}; never put a different skill in its place.\n' +
        '3. An attack that names no skill and hints at none ("I strike the rat", "I hit it", "I shoot at it"): "skill": null; the engine uses his Basic Attack. Use the Basic Attack id only when he says "basic attack".\n' +
        '4. Targets: opponents and people by their id. Resolve labels and parts of labels ("B" for "Cave Rat B"), descriptions against the catalog (wounded, ENGAGED = right in front of him, the farthest, a size or kind), pronouns, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT. A target who is not here: {"new": "<description>"}. An area skill needs no target (null). When he names no target at all, null.\n' +
        '5. A known skill used on a thing instead of a creature or person is ability_world: target = an OBJECTS or FEATURES id or {"new": "<the thing>"}; target_words = his exact words for the thing the skill acts on (not the thing he wants to affect in the end); goal = what he wants to happen, in a few of his words. Do not judge whether it can work.\n' +
        '6. Ask instead of guessing: if, after the catalog, ENGINE FACTS and RECENT, two or more readings remain that would play out differently (another target, another skill, another kind of action), answer with one clarify about that point and nothing else. If exactly one reading fits, act and do not ask.\n' +
        '7. Write what he means: in a fight, getting away from it is flee; outside a fight, getting away to another place (back the way he came, through a way he knows, to a room or place) is go, also when he calls it fleeing, backing away or withdrawing (7a and 7c say where "back" leads), and flee only when he names no place to go; stepping back or closing in during a fight is move (chasing one who runs: move "closer" with its target); looking for someone or something is activity with kind "search"; going somewhere is go; waiting is activity with kind "wait". Write every action he intends, even if it may be impossible now: the engine decides what is possible.\n' +
        '7a. Return words such as "back", "return", "the city", "where I came from" are semantic references, not names for new places. Use RECENT ROUTE and known PLACES to resolve them to an existing destination when one clearly fits; if more than one route destination genuinely fits, clarify instead of inventing a generic new place.\n' +
        `7b. go.to is the destination, never the way there. A route, path or way he names ("through the forests", "along the river", "not past the waystation", "by the coast road") is how he travels: to is the destination's known id (or {"new": "<the destination>"} when it is not known), and the route stays in his words for the story. A route is never a place of its own.\n` +
        '7b-route. A present route choice addressed to a guide ("Up the rocks on the left — we look first") is go when RECENT shows that guide just asked which way to proceed. Copy the known destination id; companions and route wording never become a new destination. A hypothetical route, a question or somebody else choosing remains no go.\n' +
        '7b. Clear present acceptance of a specific offered guidance by a PRESENT person is go to its established destination, including an adjacent room. Use the offer only to resolve his words, never as permission by itself. A drink promise, showing goods, polite assent unrelated to departure, an invitation alone or a future plan grants no movement or purchase. An unclear destination stays open; never invent one.\n' +
        '7c. In a dungeon (EXITS listed): going through a way is go with the id of the room it leads to, also when he names the door, stair or passage ("I take the stair down"); "back" or "the previous room" is the room he came from (RECENT ROUTE); a known room further on is go with its id (the engine checks the way). Going on into a part nobody has shown yet, or down where an EXITS line offers it, is go {"new": "<his words>"}. Opening or forcing what blocks a way is attempt on its FEATURES id; looking for another way is activity search.\n' +
        `7d. Setting off now is go: an announcement of his own movement in the present ("I'm heading back to the Guild hall", "I head out"), a call to set off together now ("Let's go to …", "We head out to …"; "Let's walk the lines" is go plus the activity), a deed at another place he names ("I go check the traps" is go plus the activity), following someone to a place he names ("I follow her to the hall" is go there). No movement: a question ("Shall we go ask him now?"), a proposal ending in a question mark, "whenever she is ready", "later", a wish or a plan, someone else's offer or agreement alone. Before a fight, stepping toward or back from someone here is move; getting away from a fight is flee; once he is out of it, going somewhere is go.\n` +
        '7e. Going to a room or bed of the place he is in is go there, also when he sleeps or rests there after it ("I go up to my room and sleep" is go {"new": "my room"} plus activity sleep). Resting, sleeping or waiting where he is ("I rest downstairs by the fire; I stay here") is the activity alone, no go; paying for a room is no go.\n' +
        '8-trade. buy.max_cp and sell.min_cp are TOTAL limits for the selected quantity. Convert an explicit unit rate times qty; quote that item’s exact original price clause separately. Never sum prices of unrelated items. Select only positions bought and paid for now. A bed paid now and breakfast taken/paid tomorrow are separate transactions: accept only the bed now. Copy the payment clause as its quote. For separate current item prices, keep each price with its item; do not replace them with an invented total. A later meal is not prepaid merely because he mentions it.\n' +
        '8. Several actions in one message: one command each, in his order. One trade is one buy/sell/offer.accept, including payment and goods: do not add a pay or give for it. Appraisal (check it and pay what it is worth) is sell with no agreed price, not a free give or acceptance of a provisional bid. A requested discount is a counteroffer, not acceptance of the old price. Never substitute an open offer of another party. Something he does that no command fits: other.\n' +
        '8a. A present decision to turn in for the night, spend the night sleeping, or go to bed is activity sleep (until morning when he means overnight). Buying a room is separate and does not itself book sleep. Merely finding/asking for a bed, mentioning a purpose to rest, or saying "I wake at dawn" is not an unperformed sleep action. Never infer sleep from narrator prose; the engine checks lodging and recovery.\n' +
        '9. quote: the exact words of the message the command rests on, copied as they are (do not correct the spelling).\n' +
        '10. An obstacle or danger listed in FEATURES that he deals with himself is attempt: feature = its id; aim "examine" (he looks it over, tests or studies it), "pass" (gets past, across, over, through or around it) or "disable" (disarms, opens, breaks, removes or makes it harmless); approach = his words for how; stat = what his way takes: AGI (balance, jump, dodge, slip past), STR (climb, lift, force, hold on), VIT (endure), PER (spot the safe way), INT (work out a mechanism), WIL (steel himself), or null; tool = the OBJECTS id of a thing he holds and uses for it, or null; possible = false only when his way plainly cannot work on it as the catalog describes it, else null. Looking for something hidden is activity search, never attempt.\n' +
        'FREE HELP: NPC requests, promises and paid work are ordinary world interactions. Do not create or accept private quests, attach job ids, or invent work/arrival/payment. Concrete actions use normal commands; conversation and agreement alone require no command.\n' +
        '\n' +
        'Commands for that:\n' +
        '- use_skill {skill, target}: a skill, or a plain attack, against a creature or person, or a skill on himself. skill: an id from KNOWN SKILLS | {"new": "<his name for it>"} | null; target: an id from OPPONENTS or PRESENT | {"new": "<description>"} | null (null also for a skill on himself)\n' +
        '- ability_world {skill, target, target_words, goal}: a skill used on a thing. skill as above; target: an id from OBJECTS | {"new": "<the thing>"}\n' +
        '- move {dir, target}: dir "closer" | "away"; target: an id | null\n' +
        '- flee {}: he tries to get away\n' +
        '- stealth {}: he hides here as a standalone action. For a stealth approach to movement OUTSIDE combat, keep the actual go, journey.continue or local move and add optional manner:{kind:"stealth",scope:"action"|"destination"} to THAT command instead of a competing stealth command. action means this movement is quiet; destination means travel normally and sneak only when the established destination is actually reached (go/journey.continue only). Quotes include his words for the action and its approach. A later independent hide is still a separate action, not a movement modifier. The engine decides arrival and Detection against actual observers. This grants no extra step/attack, access, success or lasting stealth mode. Do not put manner on searches, takes, skills or other actions yet, or in combat.\n' +
        '- attempt {feature, aim, approach, stat, tool, possible}: he deals with an obstacle or danger of FEATURES (rule 10)\n' +
        '- other {what}: an action of his that no command fits; what: a few of his words\n' +
        '- clarify {about, question, options}: instead of acting; about "target" | "skill" | "action"; question: one short question to the player; options: ids or short texts\n' +
        '\n' +
